| E-QUERY-032 | broken | authorization | "Sensor '{sensor_id}' is not registered for org '{org_slug}'" | No | A query explicitly scoped to a specific org (via the `clients` parameter) requested a sensor that is not registered in the `AdapterRegistry` for that org's `OrgId`. This is an **org-scoped authorization condition**, not a credential or adapter-internal failure: the adapter for `sensor_id` exists in the registry under a different org's key; the requesting org simply has no adapter for it. **Surfaced (not redacted):** unlike E-SENSOR-* errors which carry credential context and are redacted to "Internal error; see audit log", E-QUERY-032 carries no credential signal — it reports only the sensor_id (a public sensor type name, e.g., "claroty") and the org_slug (a non-secret org identifier). No credential, API key, token, or internal config detail appears in the message. The error surfaces through `map_prism_error` as MCP error code `-32602` (INVALID_PARAMS — the org+sensor combination is an invalid query scope parameter supplied by the caller). Distinct from: E-QUERY-006 ("unknown source table" — sensor name is not registered globally at all); E-SENSOR-010 (generic AdapterNotFound swallowed into partial-failure sensor_errors); E-SENSOR-060 (OrgId mismatch on adapter internal dispatch guard — routing bug, not a caller-scope condition). **When raised:** by `resolve_source_refs` in `prism-query` materialization when `clients` is non-empty, the org_slug resolves to an OrgId via OrgRegistry, but `adapter_registry.get(org_id, &sensor_id)` returns `None` (no adapter registered for this (org, sensor) pair). **Rationale for surfaced decision:** AD-017 credential opacity requires suppressing credential values and error chains that could leak auth state; E-QUERY-032 contains only public operational information (sensor type name + org slug) that the analyst already provided as query input. Surfacing this error enables the LLM agent to distinguish "wrong sensor for this org" from a transient internal failure, enabling correct retry or scope-correction behavior. **BC anchor:** BC-3.2.001 postcondition 5. |
| E-QUERY-033 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-033: limit {requested} exceeds maximum of {max} (BC-2.11.001)"` | No | Tool-level `limit` parameter exceeds the 1000 ceiling (BC-2.11.001: `limit` default 25, max 1000). Pre-execution parameter validation — semantically distinct from E-QUERY-003 (syntactic security limits), E-QUERY-005 (materialization counter), and E-QUERY-006 (scope estimate). Emitted by `PrismError::QueryLimitExceeded { requested, max }` (prism-core `error.rs`); surfaces through `map_prism_error` as MCP `-32602` INVALID_PARAMS. Message Format is the verbatim shipped display (ADR-035 canonical-row convention). History: condition shipped under E-QUERY-007 (assigned by Wave-3 pass ADV-W3MT-P58-CRIT-001, unaware of the 007 tombstone); reassigned to E-QUERY-033 per MCP cascade P2-01 adjudication 2026-06-10 (ADR-038 D5 tombstone-permanence; next sequential free code at the namespace tail). |
| E-QUERY-034 | broken | runtime | `"E-QUERY-034: query execution error: {detail}"` | No | Generic query planning/execution failure inside the engine after security validation passes: DataFusion SQL planning errors, MemTable creation/registration failures, internal-table and write-table catalog registration failures, query-runtime initialization failure, unknown storage domain, and virtual-field injection failures during materialization. `{detail}` is redacted to "<redacted; see server logs>" for DataFusion-originated errors (no upstream error chain leaks to the caller). Emitted by `PrismError::QueryExecutionFailed { detail }` (prism-core `error.rs`); surfaces through `map_prism_error` as MCP `-32000` INTERNAL_ERROR ("Internal error; see audit log") — operator-resolvable, not caller-resolvable. Not retryable (deterministic for the same query; transient resource pressure surfaces as E-WATCHDOG-001/002). History: condition shipped under E-QUERY-003, sharing one code with the syntactic security limits AND virtual-field injection failures (one-code-three-conditions, with the security-limit path double-prefixing); split per QRY cascade P5-02 adjudication 2026-06-10 (ADR-038 v1.3 — D5 family, third instance this cycle after E-CFG and E-QUERY-033). E-QUERY-003 retained by the security limits (the BC-anchored, VP-014/VP-015-proven condition with the dominant spec-citation footprint); the generic-execution condition takes the next sequential free code at the namespace tail per the established tail-allocation convention (gaps 016–019 and write-block 020–030 reservations rejected for the same reasons recorded at v1.70). **Nested-code adjudication (P6-02/P6-03, 2026-06-11):** Three conditions were flagged as embedding foreign codes in `{detail}` — adjudicated per the ADR-038 D5 family (one-code-one-condition; caller-resolvable conditions must not route through INTERNAL_ERROR):<br>**(1) E-QUERY-006 in `resolve_source_refs` (P6-02) — DIRECTED (B):** The taxonomy row for E-QUERY-006 ("Query scope too broad: estimated {count} records across {sensor_count} sensors") describes a pre-execution scope-size estimate; the `resolve_source_refs` emission describes "sensor prefix not registered in the adapter registry / table name invalid" — a structurally distinct, caller-resolvable condition (remediation: check spelling, register sensor in prism.toml). Two sites (`sensor_id_from_table_name` returns `None` arm; `adapter_registry.is_sensor_registered` false arm) both embed the E-QUERY-006 prefix in `QueryExecutionFailed.detail`, which (a) collides the two semantically distinct conditions and (b) routes caller-resolvable content to `-32000 INTERNAL_ERROR` with redacted message, losing the caller's actionable guidance. Adjudication: allocate **E-QUERY-036** (next sequential free at the namespace tail; verified zero workspace-wide matches) for "unknown source table" with a dedicated `PrismError` variant. See E-QUERY-036 row. Implementer work-order in this cascade (same branch `fix/review-2026-06-10-query-core`): add `PrismError::UnknownSourceTable { source_name: String }` to `error.rs`; flip both `resolve_source_refs` `QueryExecutionFailed` sites to the new variant; add explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm in `error_mapping.rs` (surfaced with display — caller-resolvable; `#[non_exhaustive]` fall-through must NOT be used); add/extend `error_mapping` unit test for `-32602` / `E-QUERY-036` substring; add a unit test that `resolve_source_refs` returns `UnknownSourceTable` for an unregistered sensor prefix; TD-VSDD-060 sweep: `rg 'E-QUERY-006.*unknown source\|unknown source.*E-QUERY-006' crates/` = ZERO post-fix.<br>**(2) E-ALIAS-001 in `explain.rs expand_query_with_aliases` (P6-02) — DIRECTED (B):** `PrismError::AliasNotFound { name, scope, available }` already exists with the correct E-ALIAS-001 display and correct `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` mapping in `error_mapping.rs`. The `explain.rs` site bypasses it by constructing `QueryExecutionFailed { detail: "E-ALIAS-001: alias '{alias_display}' is not defined in the alias registry" }`, routing a caller-resolvable alias-not-found condition to `-32000 INTERNAL_ERROR` with redacted message. Adjudication: the explain.rs site MUST return `PrismError::AliasNotFound { name: alias_display, scope: "alias registry".to_string(), available: String::new() }` (or populate `available` from the registry keys if accessible). No new code or variant required — `AliasNotFound` is correct. Implementer work-order (same branch): change `expand_query_with_aliases` to return `PrismError::AliasNotFound` not `QueryExecutionFailed`; update any test that asserts the old `QueryExecutionFailed`-with-E-ALIAS-001 detail to assert `PrismError::AliasNotFound` or the `-32602` path; `rg 'E-ALIAS-001.*QueryExecutionFailed\|QueryExecutionFailed.*E-ALIAS-001' crates/` = ZERO post-fix.<br>**(3) E-QUERY-009 in `QueryExecutionFailed` (P6-03) — WIRING STATUS: LATENT (unwired production path).** Adversary could not locate a live production emission site for E-QUERY-009 in prism-query/src — confirmed correct. The enforcement gate (per-sensor REQUIRED column check before API calls) is explicitly deferred to wave-5 per `extract_push_down_filters_as_map` inline comment ("Per-sensor classify_predicates integration deferred to wave-5"). The test `test_BC_2_11_007_e_query_009_error_includes_sensor_name_and_example` directly constructs `QueryExecutionFailed { detail: "E-QUERY-009: ..." }` as a structural variant-shape test — it does NOT exercise a live production code path. The taxonomy row for E-QUERY-009 is correct as defined (BC-2.11.007, DI-021). No taxonomy amendment is required now. The residual question of whether E-QUERY-009's production path should use a dedicated variant or `QueryExecutionFailed.detail` is deferred to wave-5 per DEFER-EQUERY009-001 (SESSION-HANDOFF §Drift Items) + register-burst item 8. The taxonomy-symmetry maintenance story (register-burst item 15) carries E-QUERY-009 as a first work item for wave-5 wiring; this cascade's adjudication does NOT contradict that plan (no new nesting prohibited; the pattern is simply latent, not live). |
| E-QUERY-035 | broken | validation | "Table '{sensor_id}_{table_name}' is no longer available. The sensor spec was removed." | No | A hot reload removed or invalidated this table; queries targeting it after the reload receive this structured error instead of a bare unknown-table failure. Caller remediation: re-issue against current tables or check `list_sensor_specs`. **Zero code emitters** — hot reload table unregistration is shipped (BC-2.16.007, prism-spec-engine `hot_reload.rs`) but the removed-table query path currently surfaces generic unknown-source handling; the distinct removed-after-reload error is future scope (S-3.13 dynamic-table-availability, draft; S-1.12 partial-merge — neither implementing story cites the old code). Message Format harmonized to the BC-2.16.007 pinned text ("The sensor spec was removed.") — with no shipped display, ADR-035 verbatim-display does not bind and the sole pinning BC governs; the legacy row text "...no longer available after config reload" is retired with the re-home. History: this condition held E-QUERY-011 since Phase 1 (legacy Additional Error Codes section) with zero emitters; meanwhile S-2.03 shipped `PrismError::AuditTableAccessDenied` displaying E-QUERY-011 under BC-2.15.011. Adjudicated per MCP cascade P4-05 (2026-06-10, ADR-038 v1.4 — D5 family, fourth instance this cycle): E-QUERY-011 retained by the live audit-capability condition (dominant footprint — live production emitter, MCP `-32002` mapping, merged story, integration tests pinning the substring); this condition re-homed to **E-QUERY-035**, next sequential free at the namespace tail (verified zero matches workspace-wide; gap/write-block codes rejected per v1.70 rationale). PO follow-up routed via orchestrator: BC-2.16.007 E-QUERY-011 citations ×3 → E-QUERY-035; test-vectors.md removed-spec vector → E-QUERY-035. No implementer work-order — neither condition's code changes. |
| E-QUERY-036 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-036: unknown source table '{source_name}': table is not a registered sensor or internal table. Check spelling or register the sensor in prism.toml. Available tables: [{available_tables}].{did_you_mean}"` | No | Query references a table name whose sensor prefix is not registered in the adapter registry, OR whose prefix string fails `sensor_id_from_table_name` validation (empty, invalid charset, etc.). This is a caller-resolvable condition — the user supplied an unrecognized table name; remediation is to check spelling or add the sensor to `prism.toml`. **Emitted by `PrismError::UnknownSourceTable { source_name: String, available_tables: Vec<String>, did_you_mean: Option<String> }` (variant amended by S-DEMO-PRISMQL-GRAMMAR-REMEDIATION-001 AC-021 for parity with E-QUERY-037/038/039)**, original dedicated variant per P6-02 adjudication 2026-06-11 (ADR-038 D5 family — E-QUERY-034 residual note). **AC-021 amendment (S-DEMO-PRISMQL-GRAMMAR-REMEDIATION-001):** `available_tables: Vec<String>` (pre-formatted comma-separated list of the registered sensor source prefixes / sensor IDs (e.g., `crowdstrike`, `claroty`, `armis`) at error-construction time in `resolve_source_refs`; populated via `adapter_registry.registered_sensor_ids()` — i.e., the analyst-queryable source prefixes, NOT full table names, NOT internal `prism_*` tables; may be empty when no sensors are registered; sourced from `adapter_registry` — no credential values) and `did_you_mean: Option<String>` (Levenshtein distance ≤ 3 best candidate from `available_tables` via `strsim::levenshtein`; `None` when no candidate within threshold; follows the same distance-and-phrasing convention as E-QUERY-037/038). The Display impl appends `" Available tables: [{available_tables}].{did_you_mean_suffix}"` where `did_you_mean_suffix` is either `""` (None) or `" Did you mean: '{x}'?"` (Some — leading space, one candidate, consistent with E-QUERY-037 `TableNotAvailableDetails` convention). **Label choice rationale (AC-021):** "Available tables:" selected over "Available sources:" because (a) the field is named `available_tables`, (b) E-QUERY-037 already uses "Available tables:" for the table-list label (sibling consistency), and (c) the field doc-comment in `error.rs` already describes these as "registered sensor IDs" — the label "tables" is consistent with that naming and is the analyst-facing term for the sensor source prefixes they query against. Surfaces through `map_prism_error` as MCP `-32602` INVALID_PARAMS with the full display (caller-resolvable; must NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000`). Two emission sites in `resolve_source_refs` (`prism-query/src/materialization.rs`): (a) `sensor_id_from_table_name` returns `None` (prefix invalid charset/empty); (b) `adapter_registry.is_sensor_registered` returns false (prefix valid but sensor not registered). Both were previously embedded as `QueryExecutionFailed { detail: "E-QUERY-006: unknown source table..." }` — this was a code↔row collision because the taxonomy E-QUERY-006 row means "scope too broad: estimated {count} records" (pre-execution scope estimate), a semantically different condition. E-QUERY-036 resolves the collision and restores the caller-visible error routing. History: pre-adjudication, both sites embedded `"E-QUERY-006: unknown source table..."` in `QueryExecutionFailed.detail`, routing to `-32000 INTERNAL_ERROR` with redacted message and losing the actionable "Check spelling" guidance. **Distinct from:** E-QUERY-006 (scope too broad — estimated record count ceiling); E-QUERY-032 (sensor registered globally but not for the requested org); E-QUERY-035 (table existed and was removed by hot reload); E-QUERY-037 (plan-time dynamic-availability check with additional sensor-suggestion fields). |
| E-QUERY-037 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-037: table '{table}' is not available — sensor '{sensor}' is not configured. Available sensors: [{available_sensors}]. Available tables: [{available_tables}].{did_you_mean}"` | No | **Plan-time dynamic table availability check (S-3.13). ADR-041 L4 pedagogical enrichment (v1.91): the structured error `suggestion` field MUST include "Call prism_describe('<client_id>') to see available tables and columns" plus the did_you_mean retry hint when present. See BC-2.11.017 §E-QUERY-037.** A query references a table whose sensor is not currently configured in the live `TableRegistry`. Detected at plan time (after AST parse, before fan-out) by validating each `source_ref` in the `QueryPlan` against `TableRegistry::is_registered()`. Distinct from E-QUERY-036 (materialization-time `resolve_source_refs`, no `did_you_mean`/`available_sensors` fields): E-QUERY-037 is the planner-level availability gate that fires before any sensor API contact, carries rich suggestions, and is the definitive gating error for unconfigured-sensor queries. **Message fields:** `{table}` = the full requested table name (e.g., `"armis_alerts"`); `{sensor}` = the sensor prefix extracted from the table name (e.g., `"armis"`); `{available_sensors}` = **org-scoped** comma-separated list of sensor IDs visible to the requesting client's org (see Org-Scoping Invariant below); `{available_tables}` = **org-scoped** comma-separated list of table names visible to the requesting client's org (see Org-Scoping Invariant below); `{did_you_mean}` = absent when no close match exists; present as `" Did you mean: '{best_match}'?"` (leading space, one candidate, Levenshtein distance ≤ 3 against the **org-visible** table set via `TableRegistry::did_you_mean_for_tables`). **Org-Scoping Invariant (ADR-039, SEC-001/CWE-200):** `{available_sensors}` and `{available_tables}` MUST NOT enumerate sensors or tables belonging to org(s) other than the requesting client's org scope. The three-way dispatch at error-construction time via `filter_to_org_visible`: (a) **Multi-tenant overlay deployment** — when `QueryOptions.clients` is non-empty AND `resolved_spec_map` is populated (ADR-029 per-org overlay map is present), both fields are filtered to the union of sensors and tables registered for the requesting org(s) only. (b) **Single-tenant deployment or unrestricted multi-org query** — when `QueryOptions.clients` is `None` (no client restriction) OR `resolved_spec_map` is `None` (no overlay system configured), both fields reflect the global `TableRegistry::registered_sensor_ids()` / `TableRegistry::registered_tables()` — identical to pre-ADR-039 behavior. (c) **Empty org scope** — when `QueryOptions.clients` is `Some([])` (empty slice), both fields are empty strings and `{did_you_mean}` is absent. **Suggestion contract for LLM agents:** the `available_sensors` and `available_tables` fields allow the calling LLM agent to self-correct the query without human intervention — it can immediately re-issue against a known-registered table within the same org scope. **Empty registry / single-tenant case:** when no sensors are configured globally, `available_sensors` = `""` and `available_tables` = `""`; `{did_you_mean}` is absent regardless of org scope. **MCP mapping:** surfaces as MCP `-32602` INVALID_PARAMS (caller-resolvable — configure the sensor, then retry). Not retryable without a configuration change. **Emitter:** `PrismError::TableNotAvailable(Box<TableNotAvailableDetails>)` where `TableNotAvailableDetails` holds pre-formatted `String` fields: `table: String`, `sensor: String`, `available_sensors: String` (comma-separated list, pre-formatted at construction, org-filtered), `available_tables: String` (comma-separated list, pre-formatted at construction, org-filtered), `did_you_mean: String` (empty string when absent, `" Did you mean: '{x}'?"` when present — leading space included, computed over org-visible tables only). Boxing is a justified `clippy::result_large_err` deviation: the flat inline variant with `Vec<String>` fields pushes `PrismError` over the large-error-size lint threshold; boxing the details struct keeps the enum discriminant at pointer size. The Display output is unchanged and matches the canonical Message Format exactly — only the struct shape is boxed. **`did_you_mean` implementation:** `strsim::levenshtein` (D-1163 ratified decision; `strsim` is already a workspace dependency — use it for dependency economy). Levenshtein distance ≤ 3 computed over the **org-visible** table set (via `TableRegistry::did_you_mean_for_tables(requested, &org_visible_tables)`) at error-construction time; best single candidate with lowest distance selected; absent when no candidate is within threshold. **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::TableNotAvailable` — it must NOT fall through to the `#[non_exhaustive]` catch-all `-32000`. **Distinct from:** E-QUERY-035 (table WAS registered and was subsequently removed by a hot-reload config swap — different lifecycle state); E-QUERY-036 (materialization-level `resolve_source_refs` fires without `did_you_mean`/available lists — coarser check); E-QUERY-032 (sensor registered globally but not for the requesting org). **BC anchor:** BC-2.11.001 v1.9 §Error Cases + §Postconditions (table-availability check at plan time with org-scoped enumeration; ADR-039 v1.1). |

| E-QUERY-038 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-038: column '{column}' not found in table '{table}' for client '{client_id}'; available: [{available_columns}]{did_you_mean}"` | No | **Plan-time column-availability check (ADR-041 L4).** A query references a column name that is not present in the `TableRegistry` schema for the specified table under the requesting client's `OrgId`. Fires at plan time (after E-QUERY-037 table check, before fan-out); no sensor API call is made for a rejected query. **Message fields:** `{column}` = the exact column name as written in the query; `{table}` = the table name in which the column was not found; `{client_id}` = the requesting client's org slug; `{available_columns}` = comma-separated list of column names registered in `TableRegistry` for `(table, OrgId)` at error-construction time — always present (may be empty `[]` for a table with zero registered columns); `{did_you_mean}` = absent when no close match exists; present as `" Did you mean: '{best_match}'?"` (leading space, one candidate, Levenshtein distance ≤ 3 against the available column names via `strsim::levenshtein`). **Org-scoped:** `available_columns` reflects ONLY the columns for this client's registered schema for this table; not columns from another client's sensor overlay. **Injection-safety:** `available_columns` is sourced from operator TOML specs → `TableRegistry`; column names DO NOT contain credential values, API key strings, or sensor API response data. **Emitted by:** `PrismError::ColumnNotFound(Box<ColumnNotFoundDetails>)` where `ColumnNotFoundDetails` is a `#[non_exhaustive]` struct in `prism-core/src/error.rs` carrying `{ column: String, table: String, client_id: String, available_columns: Vec<String>, did_you_mean: Option<String> }` (ADR-041; `result_large_err` boxing parity with `TableNotAvailableDetails`; see E-QUERY-037 emitter pattern). **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::ColumnNotFound` — MUST NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000`. **Gate ordering:** E-QUERY-001 (parse) → E-QUERY-037 (table exists) → E-QUERY-038 (column exists); E-QUERY-038 fires only after 037 passes. **Distinct from:** E-QUERY-036 (materialization-level unknown SOURCE TABLE, not column); E-QUERY-037 (table not in TableRegistry, one level up); E-QUERY-034 (DataFusion execution failure after the column gate). **BC anchor:** BC-2.11.016 (full postcondition spec). |
| E-QUERY-039 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-039: enrichment infusion '{infusion}' is not registered; available: [{available_infusions}]{did_you_mean}"` | No | **Plan-time enrichment-not-found gate (allocated by S-DEMO-PRISMQL-ONBOARDING-001-C/AUDIT-005; implemented by S-DEMO-FIDELITY-REMEDIATION-001).** A query references an enrichment infusion name that is not a key in `InfusionRegistry.udf_to_infusion`. Fires at plan time (after E-QUERY-038 column check), before any sensor API or infusion HTTP call is made. Applies to BOTH execution modes: (a) **Pipe-mode:** `EnrichStage.infusion` field names an unregistered infusion (pipe-mode always fires E-QUERY-039 for unregistered names — the analyst explicitly invoked an enrichment directive); (b) **SQL-mode:** `FuncCall::Scalar { ScalarFunc::Unknown(name) }` in a SELECT or WHERE position whose `name` is not a DataFusion built-in function (scalar, aggregate, or window) AND not a key in `InfusionRegistry.udf_to_infusion`. The DataFusion built-in exclusion covers all three function namespaces: scalar functions (e.g., `lower`, `upper`, `coalesce`), aggregate functions (e.g., `stddev`, `median`, `array_agg`), and window functions (e.g., `row_number`, `rank`). PrismQL's parser recognizes only 7 aggregate keywords; all other DataFusion aggregate and window function names parse as `ScalarFunc::Unknown` — the gate MUST pass them through rather than fire E-QUERY-039. The gate fires after the E-QUERY-037 table check passes (sensor is known) and the E-QUERY-038 column check passes. **Message fields:** `{infusion}` = the exact infusion name as written in the query; `{available_infusions}` = comma-separated sorted list of keys registered in `InfusionRegistry.udf_to_infusion` at error-construction time (empty list `[]` when no infusions are loaded); `{did_you_mean}` = absent when no close match exists; present as `" Did you mean: '{best_match}'?"` (leading space, one candidate, Levenshtein distance ≤ 3 against `available_infusions` keys via `strsim::levenshtein`). **`{available_infusions}` rendering rule:** the Display impl comma-joins the `Vec<String>` (sorted lexicographically) and wraps it in square brackets — e.g., `[nvd_cvss, threat_score]`; when the Vec is empty the rendered form is `[]`. This matches the `[{available_columns}]` bracket convention established by E-QUERY-038 (sibling parity). **Injection-safety:** `{available_infusions}` values are infusion IDs from operator TOML specs → `InfusionRegistry`; they DO NOT contain credential values, API key strings, or sensor API response data. **Emitted by:** `PrismError::EnrichUdfNotFound(Box<EnrichUdfNotFoundDetails>)` where `EnrichUdfNotFoundDetails` is a `#[non_exhaustive]` struct in `prism-core/src/error.rs` carrying `{ infusion: String, available_infusions: Vec<String>, did_you_mean: Option<String> }`. Boxing follows the `result_large_err` boxing parity established by `TableNotAvailableDetails` (E-QUERY-037) and `ColumnNotFoundDetails` (E-QUERY-038). **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::EnrichUdfNotFound` — MUST NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000` (caller-resolvable: register the infusion in `.prism/infusions/` or correct the name). **Gate ordering:** E-QUERY-001 (parse) → E-QUERY-037 (table exists) → E-QUERY-038 (column exists) → E-QUERY-039 (infusion registered). Enrichment-not-found fires LAST in the plan-time gate sequence; the code MUST NOT fire this gate before the E-QUERY-037 and E-QUERY-038 checks have passed. **Distinct from:** E-QUERY-037 (table not in TableRegistry); E-QUERY-038 (column not in TableRegistry schema); E-INFUSE-004 (infusion type not recognised in TOML spec — parse-time, not plan-time); E-INFUSE-006 (infusion found in registry but lookup/load failed at execution time). **BC anchor:** BC-2.11.019 v1.6 + ADR-041 v1.2 (pedagogical enrichment gate, plan-time, both pipe-mode and SQL-mode; v1.5 adds DataFusion built-in scalar exclusion — F-PJL1-HIGH-001; v1.6 expands exclusion to scalar, aggregate, and window built-ins — F1 PR-LEVEL). |

| E-QUERY-040 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-040: redundant row limit. This query caps rows in two places: a SQL \`LIMIT {sql_limit}\` in the head and a row-capping \`| limit\`/\`| tail\` pipe stage (cap: {pipe_limit}). PrismQL requires exactly one row cap. Remove the SQL \`LIMIT {sql_limit}\` and place a single \`| limit\` at the end of the pipeline (recommended for composed queries), or use \`LIMIT\` only in pure SQL-mode queries."` | No | **Plan-time FORBID-BOTH dual-limit rejection (ADR-043 D4 / HRG-1 ratified 2026-06-24; extended to cover `\| tail` pipe stage by S-DEMO-PRISMQL-GRAMMAR-REMEDIATION-001 HIGH-1).** A SQL→Pipe composed query (BC-2.11.020) specifies BOTH a SQL `LIMIT N` clause in the `SELECT … FROM t LIMIT N` head AND a row-capping pipe stage (`\| limit M` or `\| tail M`) in the tail. PrismQL requires exactly one row cap per query. **Message fields:** `{sql_limit}` = the integer `N` from the SQL `LIMIT N` clause; `{pipe_limit}` = the integer `M` from the `\| limit M` or `\| tail M` pipe stage (whichever is present). **Firing semantics:** plan-time rejection — the grammar parses the composed query successfully (both caps are syntactically valid); the plan-time validator checks the `SqlPipeQuery` node and rejects when `head.limit.is_some() && stages.contains_any(PipeStage::Limit(_) | PipeStage::Tail(_))`. Fires AFTER parse, BEFORE any DataFusion execution or sensor fan-out. **Emitted by:** `PrismError::RedundantRowLimit { sql_limit: u64, pipe_limit: u64 }` in `prism-core/src/error.rs`. **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::RedundantRowLimit` — MUST NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000` (caller-resolvable: remove one of the two row caps). **Rationale for FORBID-BOTH:** all cross-language SQL-pipe precedents (GoogleSQL pipe syntax, PRQL, KQL, Splunk SPL, Spark) structurally avoid placing two caps at the same syntactic level; for a pedagogical DSL consumed by AI agents, a clear plan-time error trains the correct idiom (`SELECT … FROM t \| enrich fn(col) \| limit N`); forbid→permit is non-breaking but permit→forbid would be a breaking change. **Acceptable future relaxation:** pipe-wins only (pipe `\| limit` becomes the final cap; SQL head `LIMIT` becomes an intermediate cap); SQL-wins is permanently ruled out (zero precedent, contradicts pipe-operator design ethos). **Gate ordering:** E-QUERY-001 (parse) → E-QUERY-040 (dual-limit plan-time check, only on `Ast::SqlPipe` nodes). **BC anchor:** BC-2.11.020 §Error Cases (full postcondition spec). **CI gate obligation (ADR-045 D3):** the shared `&'static str` example array in `resources.rs` MUST include negative-gate examples asserting that both `SELECT * FROM t LIMIT 5 \| enrich fn(x) \| limit 3` and `SELECT * FROM t LIMIT 5 \| tail 3` return `Err(PrismError::RedundantRowLimit { sql_limit: 5, pipe_limit: 3 })`. |

| E-QUERY-041 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-041: The value '{first_50_chars}' cannot be interpreted as a UTC timestamp. Expected RFC-3339 format with UTC offset (e.g., '2026-07-03T00:00:00Z'). Date-only and offset-less forms are not accepted. For relative time filters, use NOW() - INTERVAL 'Nh' (e.g., WHERE timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '24h')."` | No | **Plan-time temporal literal AST-walk error (ADR-052 §D4 — `TemporalLiteralUnparseable`).** A `Timestamp(Microsecond, Some("UTC"))` datetime column is compared against a date-like string literal. **Detection mechanism (Option A — lenient-parse-then-AST-walk, ratified 2026-07-04):** The PrismQL parser emits a `Literal::RawTemporalLiteral(String)` AST node for date-only (`'2026-06-24'`) and offset-less (`'2026-06-24T12:00:00'`) literals — parse SUCCEEDS (no E-QUERY-001 at parse time). The plan-time AST walker `check_temporal_literals` resolves each `RawTemporalLiteral` against the real schema column type with a **seven-arm dispatch** (ADR-052 §D4 v1.10): (1) `RawTemporalLiteral` vs `Timestamp(Microsecond, UTC)` column (bare `Field` LHS) → **E-QUERY-041** (this error; the primary purpose of the mechanism); (2) `RawTemporalLiteral` vs String/Utf8 column (bare `Field` LHS) → **COERCE** to `Literal::String(s)` — no error emitted, byte-identical to pre-ADR-052 behavior (`WHERE string_col = '2026-06-24'` is valid and passes); (3) `RawTemporalLiteral` vs Integer/Float/Bool column (bare `Field` LHS) → **E-QUERY-002** (`QueryTypeMismatch` — type mismatch; date-shaped literal cannot equal a numeric or boolean column); (4) `RawTemporalLiteral` in comparison position where LHS is a function or compound expression (non-`Field`) → **E-QUERY-042** (`TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition`, NonColumnLhsComparison — LHS type cannot be resolved at plan time; silent coercion would reintroduce RISK-1 for datetime-valued expressions, e.g., `WHERE to_timestamp(col) = '2026-06-24'`); (5) `RawTemporalLiteral` in SELECT projection position (bare literal in SELECT list, no column type context) → **COERCE** to `Literal::String(s)` (SUCCESS — standard SQL `SELECT '2026-06-24'` returns the string constant; OBS-2 preserved); (6) `RawTemporalLiteral` in GROUP BY position → **E-QUERY-042** (`TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition`, GroupBy — grouping by a literal constant is a degenerate no-op, almost always an analyst mistake); (7) `RawTemporalLiteral` in ORDER BY position → **E-QUERY-042** (`TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition`, OrderBy — ordering by a literal constant is a degenerate no-op, almost always an analyst mistake). **Why AST-walk, not chrono pre-validator:** The retired v1.2 design used a text-scanner over unparsed query strings (`extract_table_name_from_query_str`, `is_bad_literal_in_datetime_column`), which could not resolve dotted sources, filter-mode syntax, or qualified columns — causing Unicode byte-offset panics (VP-021 violation), dotted-column false positives, and filter-mode false positives across 8 failed fix-bursts. `check_temporal_literals` operates on the already-parsed AST with full schema resolution, eliminating all these failure modes. After the ADR-052 migration, all sensor `ColumnType::Datetime` columns are registered in the DataFusion execution schema as `DataType::Timestamp(Microsecond, Some("UTC"))` (not `DataType::Utf8`). **`{first_50_chars}`** = the first 50 characters of the offending string literal, truncated at a valid UTF-8 codepoint boundary. Truncation convention follows E-INFUSE-014 (credential exposure prevention; AD-017: credential values MUST NOT appear in query literals; the 50-char cap is a belt-and-suspenders guard against inadvertent secret exposure). **Valid RFC-3339 UTC forms** (will NOT trigger E-QUERY-041): `'2026-07-03T00:00:00Z'`, `'2026-07-03T00:00:00+00:00'`. **Invalid forms** (WILL trigger E-QUERY-041 when used in a **Datetime/Timestamp column** comparison — but will COERCE to `Literal::String` and succeed silently when used against a **String/Utf8 column**): `'2026-06-24'` (date-only, no time component), `'2026-06-24T12:00:00'` (missing UTC offset). **Non-date-like forms** (`'June 24'`, `'yesterday'`, `'not-a-date'`) are emitted by the parser as `Literal::String` (not `RawTemporalLiteral`) and are governed by ordinary type-mismatch semantics, not E-QUERY-041. **Pedagogical guidance:** the error message directs analysts to use `NOW() - INTERVAL 'Nh'` for relative time queries — the idiomatic PrismQL temporal pattern (BC-2.11.021). **Applies to:** SQL mode (`WHERE timestamp > '...'`), Pipe mode (`\| where timestamp > '...'`), Filter mode (`timestamp > '...'`). **Emitted by:** `PrismError::TemporalLiteralUnparseable { value_prefix: String }` in `prism-core/src/error.rs`, where `value_prefix` is the first 50 characters of the offending literal. The `Display` MUST produce `"E-QUERY-041: The value '{value_prefix}' cannot be interpreted as a UTC timestamp. Expected RFC-3339 format with UTC offset (e.g., '2026-07-03T00:00:00Z'). Date-only and offset-less forms are not accepted. For relative time filters, use NOW() - INTERVAL 'Nh' (e.g., WHERE timestamp > NOW() - INTERVAL '24h')."` (POL-24: byte-for-byte match required). **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::TemporalLiteralUnparseable` — MUST NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000` (caller-resolvable: fix the literal or use NOW()-INTERVAL). **Gate ordering:** E-QUERY-001 (parse) → E-QUERY-037 (table exists) → E-QUERY-038 (column exists) → E-QUERY-039 (infusion registered) → E-QUERY-041 (plan-time AST-walk `check_temporal_literals`) → DataFusion execution. **SAP-1 obligation:** if any `tracing::*!(event_type=…)` is emitted at E-QUERY-041 detection time, a corresponding row MUST be added to BC-2.16.002 Canonical Structured Event Catalog before the PR merges. **BC anchors:** BC-2.11.021 §Error Cases (temporal grammar context), BC-2.11.003 §Error Cases (SQL mode), BC-2.11.004 §Error Cases (Pipe mode). ADR-052 §D4. |
| E-QUERY-042 | broken | validation | `"E-QUERY-042: GROUP BY expects a column reference, not a literal constant. '<first_50_chars>' is a date-shaped literal — grouping by a constant has no effect and is almost certainly a query mistake. Did you mean to reference a column name, or to add a WHERE filter before grouping?"` (GroupBy position); `"E-QUERY-042: ORDER BY expects a column reference, not a literal constant. '<first_50_chars>' is a date-shaped literal — ordering by a constant has no effect. Did you mean to reference a column name that contains this value?"` (OrderBy position); `"E-QUERY-042: A date-like literal compared against a computed expression cannot be type-checked at plan time. Compare against a bare datetime column using RFC-3339 (e.g., '2026-07-03T00:00:00Z'), against a string column using a non-date-shaped value, or wrap the expression in an explicit CAST."` (NonColumnLhsComparison position) | No | **Plan-time temporal literal position/LHS-type rejection (ADR-052 §D4 v1.10 — `TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition`).** Raised by `check_temporal_literals` AST walker for three structural/positional conditions where a `Literal::RawTemporalLiteral(s)` is found in a problematic position: **(GroupBy)** A date-like literal in a GROUP BY expression (`GROUP BY '2026-06-24'`) — grouping by a bare literal constant is a degenerate no-op (every row maps to the same group keyed on the constant), almost always an analyst mistake (e.g., intended to GROUP BY a column name but accidentally quoted it); **(OrderBy)** A date-like literal in an ORDER BY expression (`ORDER BY '2026-06-24'`) — ordering by a bare literal constant is a degenerate no-op (sort order on a constant is undefined), almost always an analyst mistake; **(NonColumnLhsComparison)** A date-like literal in a comparison position where the LHS is a function call or compound expression (not a bare `Field` node, e.g., `WHERE lower(hostname) = '2026-06-24'`) — the walker cannot resolve the LHS type at plan time; silently coercing to `Literal::String(s)` would reintroduce RISK-1 for datetime-valued expressions (e.g., `WHERE to_timestamp(col) = '2026-06-24'` would compare a Timestamp against a string literal via implicit coercion); this arm closes the prior bug where `WHERE lower(hostname) = '2026-06-24'` produced `QueryPlanFailed → -32000 INTERNAL_ERROR` instead of an analyst-readable `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS`. **Distinct from E-QUERY-041:** E-QUERY-041 fires for Datetime/Timestamp column comparisons specifically (wrong literal format for a correctly-typed comparison — bare `Field` LHS is resolvable); E-QUERY-042 fires for structural/positional problems (wrong clause position, or unresolvable LHS type) regardless of column type. **MCP mapping:** `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` for all three position variants — caller-resolvable: (GroupBy) reference a column name or add a WHERE filter instead of quoting a date constant in GROUP BY; (OrderBy) reference a column name instead; (NonColumnLhsComparison) use RFC-3339 for datetime column comparisons, a non-date-shaped string for string column comparisons, or wrap the expression in an explicit CAST. **Emitted by:** `PrismError::TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition { position: TemporalLiteralPosition, value_prefix: String }` in `prism-core/src/error.rs`, where `TemporalLiteralPosition` is a new enum `{ GroupBy, OrderBy, NonColumnLhsComparison }` and `value_prefix` is the first 50 characters of the offending literal truncated at a valid UTF-8 codepoint boundary (same 50-char truncation convention as E-QUERY-041 / E-INFUSE-014; AD-017 belt-and-suspenders guard against inadvertent secret exposure). The `Display` for each position variant MUST produce the message in the Message Format column byte-for-byte (POL-24). **`map_prism_error` constraint:** must add an explicit `-32602 INVALID_PARAMS` arm for `PrismError::TemporalLiteralInvalidPosition` — MUST NOT fall through to the catch-all `-32000` (caller-resolvable for all three variants). **Gate ordering:** E-QUERY-001 (parse) → E-QUERY-041 / E-QUERY-042 (plan-time `check_temporal_literals`) → DataFusion execution; E-QUERY-041 and E-QUERY-042 can both be raised within the same `check_temporal_literals` invocation if the query has multiple predicate positions. **Pipe-mode note:** pipe `stats … by` and `sort` positions reject at parse time with E-QUERY-001 (enhanced message per ADR-052 §D4 option (a) — "pipe 'stats by' expects column references, not literal values"); `check_temporal_literals` GROUP BY and ORDER BY arms are SQL-mode only and are never reached for pipe-stage positions; `| where` non-column-LHS comparisons DO reach `check_temporal_literals` and fire the NonColumnLhsComparison arm. **SAP-1 obligation:** if any `tracing::*!(event_type=…)` is emitted at E-QUERY-042 detection time, a corresponding row MUST be added to BC-2.16.002 Canonical Structured Event Catalog before the PR merges. **BC anchors:** BC-2.11.021 §Error Cases (temporal grammar context), BC-2.11.003 §Error Cases (SQL mode), BC-2.11.004 §Error Cases (Pipe mode). ADR-052 §D4 v1.10. |

## ALIAS: Alias Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-ALIAS-001 | broken | validation | "Alias '{name}' not found in scope '{scope}'" | No | Referenced alias does not exist in any applicable scope (global or per-client) |
| E-ALIAS-002 | broken | validation | "Alias cycle detected: {chain}" | No | Creating or updating the alias would introduce a circular reference |
| E-ALIAS-003 | broken | validation | "Alias composition depth exceeded: {chain} (max 3)" | No | Alias references other aliases beyond the maximum nesting depth of 3 |
| E-ALIAS-004 | broken | validation | "Invalid parameter for alias '{name}': {reason}" | No | Alias invoked with an unknown parameter name, or a parameter value fails type validation (not a simple literal). Note: all parameters must have defaults (enforced at creation time by BC-2.11.008), so "missing parameter without default" is not a reachable state at invocation time. |
| E-ALIAS-005 | broken | validation | "Alias '{name}' has dependent aliases: {dependents}" | No | Deletion blocked because other aliases reference this alias. Delete dependents first or use `force: true` for cascade deletion. |
| E-ALIAS-006 | broken | validation | "Alias name '{name}' conflicts with reserved {type}: '{conflicting_name}'" | No | Alias name matches a known OCSF field name or PrismQL keyword. Choose a different alias name that does not shadow reserved identifiers. |

## IO: Filesystem I/O Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-IO-001 | broken | configuration | "Filesystem write failure during atomic file operation: {path} ({reason})" | No | Filesystem write failure during atomic file operation (aliases.toml, credential files). Likely caused by insufficient permissions or disk full. The operation fails entirely with no partial state. |

## SAFETY: Prompt Injection Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-SAFETY-001 | cosmetic | safety | "Suspicious pattern detected in field '{field}' of {sensor} record" | No | Informational; added to safety_flags, not a blocking error |
| E-SAFETY-002 | broken | safety | "Safety regex compilation failed at startup: {error}" | No | Fatal startup error; regex patterns invalid |

## SCHED: Scheduled Query Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-SCHED-001 | broken | not_found | "Schedule '{schedule_id}' not found" | No | Referenced schedule does not exist or has been deleted |
| E-SCHED-002 | broken | validation | "Invalid interval '{interval}': minimum is 60s" | No | Schedule interval must be at least 60 seconds to prevent excessive API load |
| E-SCHED-003 | broken | validation | "Schedule name '{name}' already exists" | No | Schedule names must be unique; use a different name or delete the existing schedule first |
| E-SCHED-004 | degraded | transient | "Max concurrent schedule executions reached ({count}/16)" | Yes | At most 16 schedules may execute simultaneously; retry after current executions complete |
| E-SCHED-005 | degraded | transient | "Previous execution of schedule '{schedule_id}' still in-flight" | Yes | The schedule's prior run has not completed; wait for it to finish or investigate if it is stuck |
| E-SCHED-006 | degraded | transient | "Query execution failed for client '{client_id}' on schedule '{schedule_id}': {reason}" | Yes | Query execution failed for a specific client; error recorded in history; schedule continues for other clients |
| E-SCHED-007 | degraded | transient | "Query exceeded watchdog limits on schedule '{schedule_id}': {reason}" | No | Scheduled query terminated by watchdog; error recorded; schedule remains active |
| E-SCHED-008 | broken | validation | "Maximum schedule count exceeded: {current_count}/{max_count}" | No | The configurable maximum number of active schedules has been reached (default 500, configurable via `[defaults.limits].max_schedules` in TOML). Delete unused schedules before creating new ones. |

| E-STORE-001 | broken | configuration | "RocksDB initialization failed: {reason}" | No | The embedded RocksDB instance could not be opened (corrupt WAL, missing directory, permissions). Requires manual intervention. |
| E-STORE-002 | broken | transient | "Domain write failed for {domain}: {reason}" | No | A write to the RocksDB storage layer failed. The operation was not persisted. Check disk space and permissions. |
| E-STORE-003 | degraded | transient | "Domain read failed for {domain}: {reason}" | Yes | A read from the RocksDB storage layer failed. May be transient (I/O contention) or permanent (corruption). |
| E-STORE-004 | broken | configuration | "Column family '{cf_name}' not found in RocksDB" | No | Expected column family is missing from the database. May indicate a schema migration issue or database corruption. |
| E-STORE-005 | broken | configuration | "Database lock held by another process at '{path}'" | No | Another Prism instance is using the RocksDB data directory; single-process invariant (DI-017) |
| E-STORE-006 | broken | configuration | "Database corruption detected at '{path}'" | No | RocksDB detected corruption; attempts automatic repair; if repair fails, requires manual re-initialization |
| E-STORE-007 | broken | configuration | "Insufficient disk space at '{path}': available {available_mb}MB, required {required_mb}MB" | No | Fatal startup error; free disk space or change state_dir path |
| E-STORE-008 | degraded | transient | "I/O error during RocksDB read for domain '{domain}': {os_error}" | Yes | Read operation failed; may be transient I/O contention or permanent disk issue |
| E-STORE-009 | broken | system | "Dirty bit write failed for operation '{op}': query aborted to preserve crash recovery safety" | Yes | Dirty bit write to RocksDB failed (disk full, I/O error). Query is aborted (fail-closed) to preserve the denylist crash recovery mechanism. Without a dirty bit, a crashing query cannot be denylisted. Retry after resolving disk/storage issue. |
| E-STORE-010 | degraded | transient | "Recovery action failed on startup for dirty bit '{key}'" | No | Warning logged; dirty bit NOT cleared; recovery retried on next startup |
| E-STORE-020 | broken | resource | "E-STORE-020: cursor cap exceeded (200 active cursors); wait for existing cursors to expire or be released" | No | Active cursor count at the cross-client cap (200). New cursor allocation rejected. Cursor expiry is automatic at 60s TTL; alternatively, complete or abandon existing cursor sessions. See BC-2.07.002 §Cursor Lifecycle (MCP-exposed surface) — Cap. |

## WATCH: Watchdog Configuration Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-WATCH-001 | broken | configuration | "Invalid watchdog level: '{value}'. Valid: normal, restrictive, permissive" | No | Fatal startup error; check `watchdog.level` in TOML config |
| E-WATCH-002 | broken | runtime | `"E-WATCH-002: watchdog restart limit exceeded for {component}: {count} restarts"` | No | Watchdog component `{component}` has restarted `{count}` times, exceeding the configured restart limit. The component will not be restarted further; the process should be treated as degraded and restarted by the operator. Emitted by `PrismError::WatchdogRestartLimitExceeded { component, count }` (prism-core `error.rs`); maps to MCP `-32000 INTERNAL_ERROR` ("Internal error; see audit log"). **Adjudication (QRY-P13-01, v1.76):** the prior taxonomy row at this slot described a config-clamp warning (see E-WATCH-003 for that condition). The `WatchdogRestartLimitExceeded` variant in `error.rs` already ships `"E-WATCH-002:"` as its display prefix — the live emitter has the dominant spec footprint. Per ADR-038 D5 (one-code-one-condition, live emitter wins over zero-emitter spec-only row): E-WATCH-002 is now assigned to the restart-limit condition; the config-clamp condition is re-homed to E-WATCH-003. |
| E-WATCH-003 | cosmetic | configuration | "Watchdog override value below safe minimum: {param}={value} (minimum {min})" | No | Config-time value clamped to minimum (64 MB memory, 5s timeout, 1000 records); warning logged. No live code emitter as of v1.76 — this is a forward-defined row for the watchdog config-clamp condition previously spec-authored at E-WATCH-002. When implemented, the watchdog config-loader must emit this code (not E-WATCH-002). **Re-homed from E-WATCH-002 (v1.76 QRY-P13-01 adjudication).** |

## WATCHDOG: Watchdog Errors

| Code | Severity | Category | Message Format | Retryable | Description |
|------|----------|----------|---------------|-----------|-------------|
| E-WATCHDOG-001 | broken | validation | `"E-WATCHDOG-001: query memory budget exceeded: limit {limit_mb}MB, used {used_mb}MB"` | No | Per-query memory budget exceeded: the query's own memory consumption tripped the per-query DataFusion memory budget (GreedyMemoryPool, 200MB default; BC-2.11.006). The query is terminated; no partial results are emitted (SessionContext dropped via RAII). Narrow the query scope or increase the per-query memory budget. Emitted by `PrismError::QueryMemoryBudgetExceeded` via `map_datafusion_memory_error` (prism-query `memory.rs`). The query itself is at fault — contrast E-WATCHDOG-002. (The pre-v1.68 denylist sentence on this row described the BC-2.15.007 watchdog model; denylisting is driven by consecutive watchdog terminations per BC-2.15.008 and is now documented on E-WATCHDOG-002.) |
| E-WATCHDOG-002 | degraded | transient | `"E-WATCHDOG-002: watchdog killed query — process RSS exceeded kill threshold ({budget_bytes} bytes budget); query token cancelled"` | Yes | Process-RSS watchdog kill: process RSS exceeded the kill threshold (95% of the 512MB process budget) on two consecutive checks (BC-2.15.007, VP-058 grace period) — typically pressure from concurrent queries; the killed query is not necessarily at fault. Retry after active queries complete, or narrow scope. Consecutive watchdog terminations are recorded for denylist evaluation (BC-2.15.008; surfaced as E-QUERY-008). Emitted by `PrismError::WatchdogKilled` (prism-storage `watchdog.rs`); pre-v1.68 the code's display collided on E-WATCHDOG-001 (P1-04, adjudicated in `proposals/cache-envelope-adjudication-2026-06-10.md`). |
| ~~E-WATCHDOG-003~~ | — | — | — | — | REMOVED — denylist case is covered by E-QUERY-008 ("Query has been denylisted..."). No duplicate code needed. |

Plugin execution panics now surface via E-PLUGIN-001. ID preserved per append_only_numbering (DF-030).** |

| E-SPEC-011 | broken | validation | "Reserved keyword '{verb}' cannot be used as a write endpoint pipe_verb. Reserved: where, sort, limit, join, enrich, head" | No | Write endpoint pipe_verb collides with a PrismQL reserved keyword |
